ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-02-SDZ3-Redna-29.ana.u163	Thank you very much, Mr Jerovšek. Finally, Mr. Penko, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-02-SDZ3-Redna-29.ana.u164	Thank you for your word. Dear Members! I'll try to answer the remaining unanswered questions. In the morning part of the debate, inter alia, it was problematic that the law concerns only officials and does not cover a sufficiently wide range of persons. In fact, the functionaries are concerned only with the part which deals with the supervision of the property status of the officials and the incompatibilities of the performance of the function with a gainful activity. To this end, the term "functioner" is also defined in Article 2. And then, in the preceding provisions, this concept is extended to some other important persons. Of course, in all the rest of the work, the law refers to everything, for example, in integrity plans, of course, it is not about respect for integrity and having only officials and so on, but about the integrity of institutions that cover all civil servants, including officials and so on. And in the final stage, the law also refers to the other side, including those who engage in possible interactions with officials, who may thus come into a corrupt relationship. Of course, they are also covered by the law when, of course, such conduct is prohibited in contact with the private sector. So that's the extent of the law. Let us not forget that we have special legislation on public servants, which specifically sets out these rules, which we bring here for officials. With regard to the lack of sanctions, I repeat that the law is not inflammatory, there is no criminal nature, and sanctions are in other regulations. Only the Public Procurement Act knows maybe 50 different offences or more. And it's so practical with all the laws. The penalty is thousands. And the criminal law, of course, has strict sanctions for corruption crimes. So that no new sanctions were necessary in this case, nor was it appropriate to prescribe. Sanctions shall exist for officials if they do not comply with the procedures provided for in the control. These sanctions shall be gradual, from a warning to a reduction in wages to a derogation from the competent authority to decide whether to terminate the mandate or any other sanctions, unless the termination of the mandate is in place in relation to, perhaps, the manner of election, the position of the individual official, etc. That is why the law is open in that part, talking about termination of the mandate and other procedures. With regard to judicial decisions relating to corruption offences, I have data, i.e. judicial statistics have been such that since 1995, 15, 1996 has been condemned. 19, 1997. 13, 1998. 16, 1999. 15 and 2000. 9 persons, no further information on legal convictions. In these cases, corruption is generally at the middle and lower levels, with a hand-to-hand bribe at this lower level. Data on the charges, perhaps for interesting reasons, in 2000 18 charges in this direction, 2001 27 and 2002 13 charges. It's from the D.A.'s office. So, it was given in a way, a criticism that there is no protection for those who report corruption. We have dealt with this and at some stage in the legislative process this idea was, but we have seen that in some way we take over this law then the powers of other institutions. When we have reviewed legislation, we have seen that such discrimination against those who, for example, report corruption is banned at the constitutional level and then drifts through different legislation. It is possible to seek justice in a court of law, it is possible to hear anonymous witnesses in a criminal procedure, etc., so the system is wide and complex and now to add something here at the principal level, probably makes no special sense. So, for this reason, I repeat, existing rules need to be known and enforced. There was a question about the status of this body, which is actually the status of Sui generis. We have also examined the matter from a constitutional point of view, we have found that there are at least two precedents from those bodies that are not listed in the Constitution, such as independent bodies such as the Court of Auditors, the Ombudsman, etc. These two precedents, based on the law, were elected by the State Audit Commission and by the Republic Commission. These are independent bodies which do not have such status in the constitution and are in fact excluded from the branch of authority. So we're essentially referring to this somewhere in this argument. Questions were raised here regarding the relationship between the Commission and the Council of the Commission, as regards membership in one body and another. I think this is more of a debate. I'd say it's, I think, consistent. It is the fact that the President of the Commission is a member of the Council for a number of reasons which he would not now mention. The other members of the Commission are not members of the Council because the other members of the Council are taxably listed. So he can't have any such ambiguity. Let us leave this discussion perhaps for individual articles, where we can still fix the matter if necessary. At first glance, I think the matter is consistently settled. With regard to the question whether it is possible now to abolish the working body of the national assembly by this regulation. I would say that this is the law proposed by the Government, the same proposer who also proposed this law, the applicable law on the incompatibilities of performing a public function with a profitable activity. This is a newer law that brings about a different institutional arrangement in this area. It does not mean, however, that in this law, it is not explicitly stated that the body of the national assembly is hereby abolished. The National Assembly can maintain this body. This law refers to transferring the powers of this body to a future commission, archives and so on. However, this body may, of course, retain another role in cases where the Commission finds irregularities and mediates matters to the competent institutions on a case-by-case basis. And it is possible to find the role of this body, which, like all other bodies, as a body of the judiciary, as a body at the level of local self-government that should be established, will not be able to exercise partial control if the concept is adopted. The concept of single control by an independent institution. Otherwise, there is probably no particular difficulty in understanding that a newer law also makes a later one in this work. I agree that, as Member, Mr Petan has opened up the problem of the police, of course, we are also aware of this problem and are always dealing with it in some way. We have informed the Prime Minister of this and have also proposed concrete measures to change the situation in this area. More than that, the Anti-Corruption Office cannot do so in this case. We can also have different views on the Ministry of Agriculture's procedures regarding the publication of the subsidy data and, of course, we advocate the transparency of any state-owned operation, but in this case I am acting on behalf of the proposer and will not continue to do so. I believe, and I conclude that this is, however, a major step forward within the scope of the options. What can be optimally done is that we also need to do something in parallel in other areas, which we have repeatedly mentioned today, that the law can also be corrected during the procedure and that it may be possible to give it more teeth than some of you express. We just did what was possible. And on behalf of the rapporteur, I propose to the Members that they support this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-02-SDZ3-Redna-29.ana.u190	Thank you very much. Dear Members and honourable Members! We have before us a proposal for a loan law with the European Investment Bank up to €34 million to finance railway projects. In the case of transport infrastructure providing reliable and cost-effective transport of goods and passengers, it is of paramount importance for each country and an important factor in general economic development. Therefore, part of the European transport policy measures is also aimed at improving transport infrastructure. In order to modernise transport infrastructure and to remove bottlenecks that allow for smooth and secure movement of passengers and goods, the European Community allocates substantial financial resources in the form of various programmes each year. One of the programmes is the Financial Instrument Ispa, which provides financial assistance to candidate countries for investment in environment and transport infrastructure between 2000 and 2006. The recipient of these funds is also the Republic of Slovenia. Funding is carried out by providing part, most of the funds from the Ispa programme, but it is partly necessary to provide resources from own resources. The following projects will be financed by the Impa funds and by today's potentially adopted law, which will allow for a later signed EIB loan agreement. It is about increasing speed on the Ljubljana-Zidani Bridge-Maribor route, the first and second stages.

ParlaMint-SI_2003-10-02-SDZ3-Redna-29.ana.u222	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear Members and Members. The Secret Data Act was adopted two years ago and some events took place at the time of implementation requiring adjustments or amendments and additions. And this amendment and amendment is before you today. This year, an initiative was launched to assess the constitutionality and legality of certain provisions, in particular Article 25 thereof, with regard to the conduct of security checks and the Constitutional Court, by a decision, suspended the implementation of this Article. This year, they were also inspected by NATO security authorities and the European Union, who made it known that they were not satisfied with the way in which security checks were carried out or with the legal solution of security checks in Slovenia, as they did not provide a guarantee that security checks would ensure that persons who should not have access to classified data were excluded. In view of these warnings and the fact that we are entering Euro-Atlantic links at the beginning of the next year, and from this point of view it is imperative that all those involved in the work of the various working bodies in these associations need permission to access classified data, we have prepared an amendment to the law which affects in particular the field of security verification. We are introducing a stage security check, namely a basic and extended security check by means of enquiries. This is essentially a concept that is represented by NATO and is exactly the same as the concepts of security verification in most European countries. In the law we also save the national security authority as the generic term we use. Therefore, the current Office of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia replaces classified information with a generic concept of a national security authority, which can, after a decision, be anyone, at the moment this is the office, but it is not necessarily that this name of the Office remains in the future. Due to the possibility of change, we have introduced a generic concept, but we have given it powers, which are also insisted upon by the NATO security authority. In view of the changed way of security checks, we have extended the circle of authorities carrying out security checks. So far, this has been done by the Ministry of the Interior’s special service for all civil servants, with the exception of the Sova employee and the Ministry of Defence. From now on, in particular as a result of this extended security inspection, we are also empowered by law to carry out security checks for police personnel. Which is also consistent with the provisions of the Police Act. The next novelty that is still proposed is a change in the deadlines for replacing the secrecy markings for documents that have been created in the past and thus the recovery of the situation that could have occurred because the documents are on different media and it is impossible for everyone to change the secret code at once. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia supports the amendments of the home working body, the Committee on Internal Policy, and we propose that the law be supported and that the full representation of representatives of the Republic of Slovenia in international organisations be allowed. Thank you.
